# Video Player
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/components/react/video-player

A video-enabled player example with floating widget mode.

<Tabs>
  <Tab title="Preview">
    <VideoPlayerDemo />
  </Tab>

  <Tab title="Code">
    ```jsx VideoPlayer.jsx theme={null}
    "use client";

    import { CloseWidgetButton } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/CloseWidgetButton";
    import { ControlBar } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/ControlBar";
    import { FeatureImage } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/FeatureImage";
    import { NextSecondsButton } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/NextSecondsButton";
    import { PlaybackRateButton } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/PlaybackRateButton";
    import { PlayerProvider } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/PlayerProvider";
    import { PlayPauseButton } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/PlayPauseButton";
    import { PrevSecondsButton } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/PrevSecondsButton";
    import { ProgressBar } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/ProgressBar";
    import { TimeIndicator } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/TimeIndicator";
    import { WidgetConsumer, WidgetProvider } from "@/registry/react/blocks/video/WidgetProvider";

    export function VideoPlayer({ vertical = false, ...args }) {
      return (
        <PlayerProvider vertical={vertical} args={{ video: true, showUserInterface: false, ...args }}>
          <WidgetProvider>
            <WidgetConsumer>
              {({ mode }) => (
                <>
                  <FeatureImage />
                  <ControlBar>
                    <PrevSecondsButton color={"white"} />
                    <span className="[&_svg]:size-14">
                      <PlayPauseButton color={"white"} />
                    </span>
                    <NextSecondsButton color={"white"} />
                  </ControlBar>
                  {mode === "widget" && (
                    <div className="fixed right-4 bottom-4 z-50 flex h-12 min-w-60 flex-row items-center gap-2 rounded-3xl bg-[#F5F5F5] p-1 shadow-md">
                      <PlayPauseButton />
                      <div className="flex grow flex-row items-center gap-2">
                        <PlaybackRateButton />
                        <PrevSecondsButton />
                        <NextSecondsButton />
                        <TimeIndicator />
                        <ProgressBar />
                        <CloseWidgetButton />
                      </div>
                    </div>
                  )}
                </>
              )}
            </WidgetConsumer>
          </WidgetProvider>
        </PlayerProvider>
      );
    }
    ```
  </Tab>
</Tabs>

## Usage

```tsx theme={null}
import { VideoPlayer } from '@/registry/react/blocks/video/VideoPlayer';

export function Player() {
  return <VideoPlayer projectId={54009} contentId="5ab6a5e1-689c-4103-962d-e7e68a4258ff" />;
}
```

`VideoPlayer` is a complete, drop-in player layout for video sources. It wraps
its own [`PlayerProvider`](/components/react/player-provider) (with `video: true`), so
you only pass `projectId` and `contentId`.

It renders a rounded feature image / video surface (536px wide, 16:9 aspect
ratio) with a control bar (skip back, play/pause, skip forward). While playing
and scrolled out of view, it detaches into a floating mini-widget with its own
compact controls, via [Widget Provider](/components/react/widget-provider).

For portrait video, see [Vertical Video Player](/components/react/vertical-video-player).


# Widget Provider
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/components/react/widget-provider

Switches the player between an inline and a floating widget layout based on visibility.

<Tabs>
  <Tab title="Code">
    ```jsx WidgetProvider.jsx theme={null}
    "use client";

    import React, { cloneElement, createContext, useContext, useState } from "react";
    import { useInView } from "react-intersection-observer";

    import { usePlayerSelector } from "@/registry/react/PlayerProvider";

    export const WidgetContext = createContext(null);

    export const useWidgetContext = () => {
      const context = useContext(WidgetContext);
      if (!context) {
        throw new Error("useWidgetContext must be used within a WidgetProvider");
      }
      return context;
    };

    export function WidgetConsumer({ children }) {
      const context = useWidgetContext(WidgetContext);
      return children(context);
    }

    export function WidgetProvider({ children }) {
      const playbackState = usePlayerSelector((p) => p.playbackState);
      const [ref, inView] = useInView({ threshold: 0 });
      const [closed, setClosed] = useState(false);
      const mode = inView || closed || playbackState === "stopped" ? "inline" : "widget";
      const close = () => setClosed(true);
      return (
        <WidgetContext.Provider value={{ mode, close }}>
          {cloneElement(React.Children.only(children), { ref })}
        </WidgetContext.Provider>
      );
    }
    ```
  </Tab>
</Tabs>

## Installation

<CodeGroup>
  ```bash npm theme={null}
  npx @beyondwords/cli components add widget-provider
  ```

  ```bash pnpm theme={null}
  pnpm dlx @beyondwords/cli components add widget-provider
  ```

  ```bash yarn theme={null}
  yarn dlx @beyondwords/cli components add widget-provider
  ```

  ```bash bun theme={null}
  bunx --bun @beyondwords/cli components add widget-provider
  ```
</CodeGroup>

## Usage

Wrap a single element in `WidgetProvider` and read the `mode` with
`WidgetConsumer` (a render prop) or the `useWidgetContext` hook.

```tsx theme={null}
import { PlayerProvider } from '@/registry/react/PlayerProvider';
import { WidgetProvider, WidgetConsumer } from '@/registry/react/WidgetProvider';

export function Player() {
  return (
    <PlayerProvider args={{ projectId: 9504, contentId: "ba00ef51-03ac-4a85-8b98-e45c957e8ef8", showUserInterface: false }}>
      <WidgetProvider>
        <WidgetConsumer>
          {({ mode, close }) => (
            <div className={mode === 'widget' ? 'fixed right-4 bottom-4' : ''}>
              {/* your controls */}
              {mode === 'widget' && <button onClick={close}>×</button>}
            </div>
          )}
        </WidgetConsumer>
      </WidgetProvider>
    </PlayerProvider>
  );
}
```

`WidgetProvider` tracks whether its child is on screen (via
`react-intersection-observer`). It exposes:

* **`mode`**: `"inline"` while the child is in view, the player is `stopped`,
  or the widget was closed. Otherwise `"widget"` (e.g. a floating mini-player
  once the user scrolls past it).
* **`close()`**: pins `mode` back to `"inline"` permanently.

`WidgetProvider` clones its single child to attach the visibility `ref`, so it
must wrap exactly one element. Read the value lower in the tree with
`useWidgetContext()`, which throws if used outside a `WidgetProvider`.

## Reference

|                       |                                                                                                                      |
| --------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Exports**           | `WidgetProvider`, `WidgetConsumer`, `useWidgetContext`                                                               |
| **Provides**          | `mode` (`"inline"` or `"widget"`), `close()`                                                                         |
| **Player state read** | `playbackState`                                                                                                      |
| **Requires**          | a [`PlayerProvider`](/components/react/player-provider) ancestor, and `WidgetProvider` must wrap exactly one element |


# Authentication
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/admin/authentication



BeyondWords offers multiple authentication methods to help members access their accounts securely and conveniently. Depending on your organization’s setup, you can log in using a magic link, sign in with Google, SAML SSO, or multi-factor authentication.

## Magic link

A magic link is a secure login link sent to your email address, which allows you to sign in without using a password.

To log in with a magic link:

1. Go to the BeyondWords login page
2. Enter your email address
3. Click **Continue with email**
4. Click the magic link in your email

<Warning>
  Magic links are only valid for 10 minutes.
</Warning>

## Sign in with Google

Sign in with Google allows you to log in securely using your Google account, without a password or magic link.

To log in with Google:

1. Go to the BeyondWords login page
2. Click **Sign in with Google**
3. Select your Google account and follow the sign-in prompts

## SAML SSO

SAML SSO (Security Assertion Markup Language Single Sign-On) allows you to securely log in using your organization’s identity provider (IdP), such as Okta or Azure AD.

This option is available to users on Enterprise plans.

### Set up SAML SSO

<Steps>
  <Step title="Request SAML SSO">
    [Reach out](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support) to request SAML SSO to be enabled.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Receive your setup link">
    We’ll share a unique configuration URL where you can connect your IdP. We support SAML 2.0-compliant providers including:

    * Okta
    * Microsoft Entra ID
    * OneLogin
    * LastPass
    * Auth0
    * Bitium
  </Step>

  <Step title="Configure the connection">
    Follow your identity provider’s documentation to complete the SAML configuration using the URL we provide. If needed, we’re happy to assist with setup or troubleshooting.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Provision members in your IdP">
    Ensure all members are added to your IdP and assigned to the BeyondWords SAML application. Otherwise, they won't be able to log in.
  </Step>
</Steps>

<Warning>
  Once SAML SSO is enabled, all members of your organization will be required to log in via your identity provider. Other login methods will be disabled.
</Warning>

### Log in with SAML SSO

To log in with SAML SSO:

1. Go to the BeyondWords login page
2. Click **Continue with SAML SSO**
3. Enter your email address
4. Click **Continue with SAML**
5. Follow the instructions to log in through your identity provider

## Multi-factor authentication (MFA)

Multi-factor authentication (MFA) adds an extra layer of security by requiring a second verification step when logging in.


# Credits and billing
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/admin/credits-and-billing



Learn how credits and billing work in BeyondWords.

## Credits

Credits are the unit of measurement for audio and video generation in BeyondWords.

Your monthly credit allowance depends on your plan, and credits are shared across all projects in your organization.

You can view your remaining credit balance in the bottom-left corner of any project dashboard.

<Warning>
  If your organization is on a legacy plan, your usage may be measured differently and overage may not be available. [Contact support](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support) for details.
</Warning>

### Credit costs

| Content | Credit cost            | Approximate equivalent     |
| ------- | ---------------------- | -------------------------- |
| Audio   | 1 credit per character | \~1,000 credits per minute |
| Video   | 50 credits per second  | \~3,000 credits per minute |

<Tip>
  You can track credit usage in detail with usage analytics.
</Tip>

### Rollover policy

Unused credits roll over for up to two months, provided you don't downgrade or cancel your plan.

Your monthly credit allowance resets at the start of each billing month.

Deleting audio or video does not restore credits.

### Regeneration costs

When content is regenerated, BeyondWords only regenerates segments that have changed.

You are only charged credits for regenerated segments.

### Overage

Both Standard and Enterprise plans support pay-as-you-go overage.

To manage overage settings:

1. Go to **Organization → Subscription → Overage**
2. Enable or disable overage
3. Optionally set an overage limit
4. Click **Save changes**

If overage is enabled, media generation will continue after your monthly credit allowance is reached until the overage limit (if set) is reached.

Overage is charged per 1,000 credits, with rates based on your total usage during the billing period.

If overage is disabled, media generation will stop once your monthly credit allowance is reached and no additional charges will be incurred.

<Warning>
  Content skipped due to insufficient credits will not automatically generate once your credits reset.
</Warning>

## Billing

### Manage billing

To manage billing:

1. Go to **Organization → Subscription**
2. Click **Manage billing**

You will be redirected to Stripe, where you can:

* Update your payment method
* Update your billing information, including your email address, company address, phone number, and Tax ID
* View your invoice history

### Change your subscription

To upgrade, downgrade, cancel your subscription, [contact support](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support).

## FAQs

<AccordionGroup>
  <Accordion title="Are we charged every time someone plays audio or video?">
    No, you are not charged every time someone plays audio or video. You are only charged credits for \*\*generating \*\*audio/video.
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="If I regenerate an article, am I charged for the whole thing?">
    If you regenerate an article, you are only charged for the segment or segments that need to be regenerated. For example, if you correct a typo, you will only be charged for regenerating the paragraph containing that typo.
  </Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>


# Members
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/admin/members



BeyondWords lets you add members to your [organization](/docs-and-guides/admin/organization) so you can collaborate on [projects](/docs-and-guides/get-started/projects). You can assign roles to control what each member can view and manage.

The number of members you can add depends on your plan.

## Add a member to an organization

<Steps>
  <Step title="Start an invitation">
    Go to **Organization** → **Members** in your BeyondWords dashboard.

    Then, click the **Invite** button.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Start an invitation">
    Go to **Organization → Members** in your BeyondWords dashboard.

    Then, click the **Invite** button.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Add member details">
    Add the following details about your new member:

    * **Email**
    * **First name**
    * **Last name** (optional)
    * **Role**: Determines what they can view and manage within accessible projects—[learn more below](#add-a-role)
    * **Profile picture** (optional)
    * **Project access**: Determines which project(s) they can access
  </Step>

  <Step title="Add member details">
    Add the following details about your new member:

    * **Email**
    * **First name**
    * **Last name** (optional)
    * **Role**: Determines what they can view and manage within accessible projects—[learn more below](#add-a-role)
    * **Profile picture** (optional)
    * **Project access**: Determines which projects they can access
  </Step>

  <Step title="Add member details">
    Add the following details about your new member:

    * **Email**
    * **First name**
    * **Last name** (optional)
    * **Role**: Determines what they can view and manage within accessible projects—[learn more below](#add-a-role)
    * **Profile picture** (optional)
    * **Project access**: Determines which projects they can access
  </Step>

  <Step title="Send the invite">
    Click **Send invite**.

    The invitee will receive an email with a link to create their profile.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Send the invite">
    Click **Send invite**.

    The invitee will receive an email with a link to create their profile.
  </Step>
</Steps>

<Warning>
  If your organization uses [SAML SSO](/docs-and-guides/admin/authentication#saml-sso) to log into BeyondWords, ensure the user is also added to your identity provider and assigned to the BeyondWords SAML application. Otherwise, they won't be able to log in.
</Warning>

<AccordionGroup>
  <Accordion title="View existing members">
    To view existing members, go to **Organization → Members** in your BeyondWords dashboard.

    You can see their names, email addresses, roles, and joined dates at a glance.

    Use the search bar to find a specific member.
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="Edit a member">
    To edit a member:

    1. Go to **Organization → Members** in your BeyondWords dashboard
    2. Click **••• → Edit** alongside the member you want to edit
    3. Make changes and click **Save changes**
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="Delete a member">
    To delete a member:

    1. Go to **Organization → Members** in your BeyondWords dashboard
    2. Click **••• → Delete** alongside the member you want to delete
    3. Click **Delete** to confirm
  </Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

## Add a member to a project

When a [project](/docs-and-guides/get-started/projects) is created, only the project creator and organization owner have access by default.

To add more members to a project:

1. Go to **Settings → Members** in the project dashboard
2. Click **+ Member**
3. Click the names of any members you want to add

Alternatively, go to **Organization → Members** and manually edit the member's project access.

<Accordion title="Remove a member from a project">
  To remove a member from a project:

  1. Go to **Settings → Members** in the project dashboard
  2. Click **•••** alongside the member you want to remove
  3. Click **Remove from project**

  Alternatively, go to **Organization → Members** and manually edit the member's project access.
</Accordion>

## Add a role

A member's role determines what they can view and manage within accessible projects. You can create new roles to provide the exact permissions you need.

<Steps>
  <Step title="Start a new role">
    Go to **Organization** → **Members** in your BeyondWords dashboard.

    Open the **Roles** tab and click **+ Role**.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Add role details">
    Give your role a name, then set its permissions for the following dashboard areas:

    Organization, members, roles, analytics, projects, content, pronunciations, scripts, video, background music, filters, integrations, player, playlists and podcast feeds, ads, and custom voices.

    For each area, choose one of the following:

    * **No access**: Can’t view
    * **Read**: Can view, but can’t make changes
    * **Read and write**: Can view and make changes
  </Step>

  <Step title="Save the role">
    Click **Save changes**.

    The role now appears in the table and can be assigned when inviting or updating members.
  </Step>
</Steps>

<AccordionGroup>
  <Accordion title="View existing roles">
    To view existing roles, go to **Organization → Roles** in your BeyondWords dashboard.

    You can see how many members are assigned to each role at a glance. Click any role to view its permissions.
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="Edit a role">
    To edit a role:

    1. Go to **Organization → Roles** in your BeyondWords dashboard
    2. Click ••• **→ Edit** alongside the role you want to edit
    3. Make changes and click **Save changes**

    Please note that role changes will apply to any members that already have this role.
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="Delete a role">
    To delete a role, first remove any members assigned to it by deleting them or reassigning them to another role. Then:

    1. Go to **Organization → Roles** in your BeyondWords dashboard
    2. Click **••• → Delete** alongside the role you want to delete
    3. Click **Delete** to confirm
  </Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>


# Organization
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/admin/organization



Each BeyondWords subscription is linked to a single organization, which can contain multiple [projects](/docs-and-guides/get-started/projects) and [members](/docs-and-guides/admin/members), depending on your plan.

## Organization settings

To access your organization settings, click your avatar → **Organization**. There, you can:

* Update your organization's information
* View usage analytics
* Manage members
* Create and manage custom voices
* Manage your subscription

## Update your organization's information

You can update your organization's name, legal name, website, and address in **Organization → General**. These details are used for your records and may be visible to your team.

Once complete, click **Save changes**.

## Create and manage custom voices

You can add and manage custom voices ([generated](/docs-and-guides/voices/voice-generation) and [cloned](/docs-and-guides/voices/voice-cloning) voices) in **Organization → Custom voices**.

To create a new custom voice, click **+ Voice** and follow the instructions.

The custom voices table shows details about your existing custom voices:

* **Voice**: The name of the voice
* **Speaker**: The speaker associated with this voice
* **Type**: The type of voice (Generated, Professional (voice clone), or Instant (voice clone))
* **Language**: The voice's primary language (and optional accent)
* **Status**: Enabled (ready to use), Processing, Draft
* **Date**: The date the voice was created

The **•••** icon alongside each voice gives you access to three options:

* **View**: Preview how your voice sounds across different models, languages, and accents. You can also change project access to this voice.
* **Copy ID**: Copy the voice ID to use with the API
* **Delete**: Permanently delete the custom voice from your organization

## Manage your subscription

You can view and manage your subscription in **Organization → Subscription**.

* Click **Manage billing** to manage your billing details and invoices through Stripe
* Click **Cancel subscription** to initiate the cancellation process

## Transfer your organization to another owner

To change the owner of your organization, please [contact support](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support).


# Profile
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/admin/profile



Your profile contains your member information, notification settings, and connected apps.

## General

To update your profile's general settings (name, email, profile picture):

<Steps>
  <Step title="Go to your profile">
    Click your profile picture → **Profile**.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Update general settings">
    In the **General** section, you can update the following information:

    * **First name**: Visible to other members
    * **Last name (optional)**: Visible to other members
    * **Email**: Used to log into your account
    * **Profile picture (optional)**: If not provided, your first name initial will be used as your profile icon

    <Note>
      You can view your assigned role in your profile. Members with the appropriate permissions can update roles in **Organization** → **Members**.
    </Note>
  </Step>

  <Step title="Save changes">
    Once complete, click **Save changes**.
  </Step>
</Steps>

## Notifications

To update your BeyondWords notification settings:

1. Click your profile picture → **Profile**
2. Go to the **Notifications** section
3. Check/uncheck the relevant boxes
4. Click **Save changes**

## Connected apps

You can connect your BeyondWords account to an AI app using the BeyondWords MCP.

Once connected, you can use the assistant to configure and manage your BeyondWords account, projects, and content through conversation.

For example, you can:

* **Manage projects and content**: View and update projects, create and manage content, regenerate audio, and more
* **Explore analytics**: Ask about performance across your organization, projects, and individual content
* **Manage pronunciation rules**: View, create, and update rules that control how words and phrases are pronounced
* **Explore and manage voices**: Find available voices, view voice details, and manage the voices used in your projects
* **Check languages and player settings**: See which languages are available and review your player configuration

Some actions only retrieve information, while others can make changes to your BeyondWords account and require additional permission.

### Connect an app to your account

To connect an AI app to your BeyondWords account:

<Steps>
  <Step title="Go to your profile">
    Click your profile picture → **Profile**.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Start a new connection">
    Go to **Connected apps** in the left-hand sidebar, then click **+ Connect**.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Choose and connect an app">
    Choose which AI app you'd like to connect your account to, then follow the instructions to complete the connection:

    <AccordionGroup>
      <Accordion title="Claude">
        A paid Claude plan is required to connect your Claude account to your BeyondWords account.

        **Pro and Max plans:**

        1. Copy the MCP server URL provided in BeyondWords
        2. In Claude, go to **Settings → Connectors**
        3. Click **+ → Add custom connector**
        4. Enter a name for the connector, such as "BeyondWords"
        5. Paste the remote MCP server URL into the relevant field
        6. Click **Add**

        **Team and Enterprise plans:**

        First, someone with the Owner or Primary Owner role in Claude must add the custom connector to your organization:

        1. Copy the MCP server URL provided in BeyondWords
        2. In Claude, go to **Organization settings → Connectors → Add**
        3. Hover over **Custom**, then click **Web**
        4. Enter a name for the connector, such as "BeyondWords"
        5. Paste the remote MCP server URL into the relevant field
        6. Click **Add**

        Once the connector has been added to your organization, members can connect to BeyondWords:

        1. In Claude, go to **Customize → Connectors**
        2. Find the **BeyondWords** connector and click **Connect**
      </Accordion>

      <Accordion title="Claude Code">
        To connect your BeyondWords account to your Claude Code account:

        1. Copy the command provided in BeyondWords
        2. Paste and run the command in your terminal
        3. Run **/mcp** inside Claude Code
      </Accordion>

      <Accordion title="ChatGPT">
        To connect your ChatGPT account to your BeyondWords account:

        1. Copy the MCP server URL provided in BeyondWords
        2. Click the the **Open ChatGPT settings** link provided in BeyondWords
        3. In the ChatGPT menu, scroll down to **Developer mode**, then use the toggle to enable **Developer mode**
        4. Close the dialog, select **Plugins** from the left-hand menu, then click **+**
        5. In the Name field, enter "BeyondWords"
        6. In the Connection field, paste the MCP server URL
        7. Check the box to confirm that you approve the BeyondWords server, then click **Create**
      </Accordion>

      <Accordion title="VS Code">
        To connect your Visual Studio Code account to your BeyondWords account: 

        1. Follow the **Add to VS Code** link provided in BeyondWords
        2. Confirm the server
        3. Sign in when prompted by Copilot
      </Accordion>

      <Accordion title="Cursor">
        To connect your Cursor account to your BeyondWords account: 

        1. Follow the **Add to Cursor** link provided in BeyondWords
        2. Confirm the server
        3. Sign in from the MCP settings
      </Accordion>

      <Accordion title="Other apps (MCP clients)">
        To connect your BeyondWords account to another AI app:

        1. Copy the MCP server URL provided in BeyondWords
        2. In your AI app, look for the option to add a custom connector,  plugin, or MCP server
        3. Follow the instructions provided by your app, pasting the MCP server URL into the relevant field

        The BeyondWords MCP uses Streamable HTTP for remote connections. If your app doesn’t support remote MCP servers using Streamable HTTP, it isn’t compatible with the BeyondWords MCP.
      </Accordion>
    </AccordionGroup>
  </Step>

  <Step title="Complete authentication">
    You’ll be asked to sign in to BeyondWords and choose which permissions to grant. We recommend granting both permissions for full access to the BeyondWords MCP.

    Once complete, the connection is ready to use.

    To test the connection, ask your AI assistant a question about your BeyondWords account, projects, or content.
  </Step>
</Steps>

## FAQs

<AccordionGroup>
  <Accordion title="How do I reset my password?">
    To reset your password:

    1. Log out of your account or go to the login page
    2. Enter your email address
    3. Enable the **Sign in with password** toggle
    4. Click **Forgot password?**
    5. Follow the instructions in the email you receive
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="How do I log into my account?">
    To log into your account, go to the BeyondWords login page and choose one of the following methods:

    * **Sign in with Google**: Click to continue with your Google account
    * **Continue with SAML SSO**: Sign in through your Identity Provider (IdP)
    * **Email (magic link)**: Enter your email address then click **Continue with email**. You'll receive a login link via email.
    * **Sign in with password**: Enable the toggle, enter your email address and password, then click **Log in**

    [Learn more about authentication →](/docs-and-guides/admin/authentication)
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="How do I log out of my account?">
    To log out of your account, click your profile picture → **Log out**.
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="How is my content used by connected AI apps?">
    Your AI assistant can only access BeyondWords data and actions made available through the BeyondWords MCP. What it can access and whether it can make changes depends on the permissions you grant when connecting your account. You can grant read-only access or also allow supported actions such as creating, updating, and deleting content.

    Information retrieved from BeyondWords may become part of your conversation with the AI app. How that data is stored and used depends on the AI provider, your plan, and your settings.

    Connecting your account does not make your BeyondWords content available to other users.
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="How are app connections secured?">
    The BeyondWords MCP uses OAuth to authenticate your account and manage permissions. You sign in directly to BeyondWords and choose which permissions to grant, so you don't need to share your BeyondWords password with the AI app.
  </Accordion>

  <Accordion title="How do I disconnect an AI app?">
    To disconnect an AI app from your BeyondWords account:

    1. In BeyondWords, click your profile picture → **Profile**
    2. Go to **Connected apps**
    3. Click **Revoke** alongside the app you want to disconnect
  </Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>


# Usage analytics
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/admin/usage-analytics



BeyondWords provides usage analytics so you can track how your plan is being used and monitor activity across projects.

## Check usage analytics

<Steps>
  <Step title="Open usage analytics">
    Go to **Organization** → **Usage** in your BeyondWords dashboard.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Choose a metric">
    Choose which metric to check:

    <Warning>
      The available metrics depend on your plan and billing type.
    </Warning>

    * **Unique articles**: The number of content items processed. Each item is counted once, even if it generates multiple audio and/or video outputs.
    * **Credits**: Credits used across audio and video generation
    * **Characters**: Total characters processed across articles and scripts, including letters, numbers, spaces, and special characters. Each character is counted once, even if used for both audio and video generation.
    * **Billed articles**: The number of articles that counted towards billing
  </Step>

  <Step title="Choose a date range">
    Choose whether to view data for a particular billing period, week, month, or custom date range.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Choose granularity">
    Choose whether to view data by hour, day, week, month, or year, or as a cumulative total.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Choose a breakdown (optional)">
    Optionally filter data for specific voices, projects, or content items.
  </Step>

  <Step title="Choose chart type">
    Use the dropdown menu to switch between a bar chart and line chart.
  </Step>

  <Step title="View your data">
    You can now view your selected data in the chart.

    Hover over a bar or point to see the exact value.
  </Step>
</Steps>


# Analytics dashboard
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/analytics/analytics-dashboard



Use the player analytics dashboard to understand how users engage with your [audio](/docs-and-guides/content/audio) and [video](/docs-and-guides/content/video) content.

* If you're using the [BeyondWords player](/docs-and-guides/distribution/player/player-introduction), analytics are tracked in the dashboard by default. (You can enable or disable this setting in the **Analytics → Preferences** section of your [project dashboard](/docs-and-guides/get-started/projects).)
* If you're using a custom player, you can [send analytics events to BeyondWords](#send-analytics-to-beyondwords) and view them in the same dashboard.

<Tip>
  Prefer to view your metrics elsewhere? Learn about [analytics integrations](/docs-and-guides/analytics/analytics-integrations).
</Tip>

## View the analytics dashboard

To view the analytics dashboard, open your [BeyondWords project](/docs-and-guides/get-started/projects) and go to **Analytics → Player**.

Use the filters at the top to control what data is displayed:

* **Format**: Audio only, video only, or both formats
* **Content type**: <Tooltip>Articles</Tooltip> only or <Tooltip>summaries</Tooltip> only
* **Access tier**: All [access tiers](/docs-and-guides/distribution/player/access-tiers), or choose the slug for a specific access tier
* **Device**: Whether the user was experiencing the player through desktop, mobile web, tablet web, the iOS SDK, or Android SDK
* **Date range**: Only include analytics events that occurred within the selected time period (e.g., "All time", "Last 28 days", or "23 Apr '26 – 11 May '26")

## Project metrics

You can view the following [project](/docs-and-guides/get-started/projects) metrics in your analytics dashboard:

* **Plays**: The number of playback sessions where audio or video started. We count one play when a user starts playback for a piece of content within a player session, not every time they pause and resume.
* **Engagement rate**: The percentage of player loads that generated a play
* **Unique users**: The approximate number of distinct browser profiles that played audio or video. Users are identified using an anonymous ID stored in browser localStorage, when analytics storage is enabled. The same person may be counted more than once across browsers, devices, domains, or after clearing browser storage. (You can enable or disable tracking in the **Analytics** → **Preferences** section of your project dashboard.)
* **Playback time**: The total time spent listening to audio or watching video across all playback sessions
* **Loads**: The number of times the player loaded with playable audio or video content available. We count one load per player session.
* **Avg. playback duration**: The average percentage of audio or video content consumed per playback session
* **Avg. playback time**: The average time spent listening to audio or watching video per playback session
* **Drop-off analysis**: The percentage of listeners/viewers who reached each 10% playback interval
* **Device usage**: The breakdown of plays by device type

In most cases, the current metric appears in bold black text. A red or green indicator shows how the metric compares to the previous equivalent time period, while the smaller gray text shows the previous metric value.

Line graphs show how metrics changed over the selected time period. Click a graph to view it in more detail, and hover over the line to see values for specific points in time.

## Content metrics

The table at the bottom of the player analytics dashboard shows key metrics (plays, engagement, average playback time, and average playback duration) for individual [content items](/docs-and-guides/content/content-items). You can sort results based on any of these metrics.

Use the search bar to look up a specific content item.


# Analytics integrations
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/analytics/analytics-integrations



BeyondWords analytics events can be sent to multiple destinations, allowing you to track user engagement wherever is convenient.

If you're using the [BeyondWords player](/docs-and-guides/distribution/player/player-introduction), events are sent to the [analytics dashboard](/docs-and-guides/analytics/analytics-dashboard) by default. You can also send events to Google Analytics or a custom analytics endpoint.

If you're using a custom player, you can send events to BeyondWords so they appear in the analytics dashboard.

## Send analytics to Google Analytics

To send events from the BeyondWords player to a configured Google Analytics account:

1. Go to **Analytics → Preferences** in your [project dashboard](/docs-and-guides/get-started/projects)
2. Enable **Send data to Google Analytics**
3. Enter your Google Analytics Measurement ID or Google Tag Manager Container ID in the **Identifier** field
4. Click **Save changes**

## Send analytics to a custom endpoint

To send events from the BeyondWords player to a custom endpoint:

1. Go to **Analytics → Preferences** in your BeyondWords dashboard
2. Enter the custom analytics URL to send player events to
3. Click **Save changes**

The BeyondWords player will send analytics events to your endpoint as JSON `POST` requests using the [event schema](#event-schema) below.

## Send analytics to BeyondWords

To send analytics from a custom player to BeyondWords, send a `POST` request to `https://metrics.beyondwords.io` using the [event schema](#event-schema) below. We recommend sending as many fields as possible so events can be aggregated accurately, although sensible defaults will be applied for some missing fields.

Some fields may not apply to your implementation. These can be omitted and will be ignored.

When testing custom events, it may take up to an hour for data to appear in the BeyondWords dashboard. If you need help, [contact support](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support).

## Example event

The following example shows a typical analytics event payload. To inspect analytics events, search for `metrics.beyondwords.io` in your browser's Network tab.

```javascript Example event theme={null}
{
  "event_type": "play",
  "device_type": "desktop",
  "media_type": "content",
  "media_variant": "article",
  "project_id": 123,
  "content_id": "3f57001d-cb30-42c1-ad6f-047e813c360f",
  "source_id": "example-source-id",
  "analytics_id": 333,
  "ad_id": null,
  "media_id": 555,
  "media_format": "audio",
  "local_storage_id": "bef9218d-3871-450e-a7fa-5da65102f532",
  "listen_session_id": "839ff785-0cb8-4e74-a061-506f571c79ce",
  "session_created_at": 1672531200000,
  "duration": 123.45,
  "listen_length_seconds": 0.2,
  "listen_length_percent": 0.1620089105,
  "speed": 1,
  "segment_playback_enabled": true,
  "location": "https://example.com",
  "referrer": "https://example.com",
  "player_version": "1",
  "player_npm_version": "1.2.3"
}
```

## Event schema

<ParamField type="string">
  The type of analytics event. There are four types:

  * **load**: content loaded successfully and the media's duration is known
  * **play**: the media started playing for the current content item
  * **play\_progress**: playback reached the next 10% of the audio's duration
  * **ad\_link\_click**: the user clicked on the click-through URL for an advert
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  The type of device that emitted the event. There are three types:

  * **phone**: the width of the browser was between 0px and 499px
  * **tablet**: the width of the browser was between 500px and 999px
  * **desktop**: the width of the browser was 1000px or greater
  * **ios**: the event was sent from a native ios app (e.g. )
  * **android**: the event was sent from a native android app (e.g. )
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  The type of media loaded in the player. There are two types:

  * **content**: the event was emitted during content playback
  * **ad**: the event was emitted during advert playback
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  The variant of media loaded in the player. There are two types:

  * **article**: the media is for the original article
  * **summary**: the media is for a summary of the article
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="integer | null">
  The numeric ID of the project whose content is loaded into the player. This field might be null if content wasn't fetched from the API and is instead set manually in the player.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string | integer | null">
  The string UUID or numeric ID of the content currently loaded into the player. This field might be null if content wasn't fetched from the API and is instead set manually in the player.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string | null">
  The string ID that can optionally be associated with the content currently loaded into the player. This field might be null if the source\_id ID wasn't set in the BeyondWords API.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="integer | null">
  A numeric ID used by BeyondWords for associating this event with your project. This field might be null if content wasn't fetched from the API and is instead set manually in the player.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="integer | null">
  The numeric ID of the advert that is currently loaded into the player. This field is null when an advert isn't playing, i.e. when content is playing.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="integer | null">
  The numeric ID of the content or advert media field that is currently loaded into the player. This field is null when VAST adverts are playing.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  The format of media loaded in the player. There are two formats:

  * **audio**: the player is playing audio (or will play audio)
  * **video**: the player is playing video (or will play video)
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string | null">
  A string UUID associated with the current user and stored in local storage under the 'beyondwords' key. This field is null if advertConsent is set to 'without-local-storage'.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  A string UUID associated with the current player instance. This ID changes after the page is reloaded and is different for each player instance.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="integer">
  The unix time in milliseconds at which the first analytics event was sent from the current player instance, as reported by the user's browser.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="float">
  The duration in seconds of the content or advert media currently loaded into the player.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="float">
  The duration in seconds that the user has listened to of the currently loaded media.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="float">
  The percentage that the user has listened to of the currently loaded media.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="float">
  The speed at which the user is listening to the media, e.g. 0.5, 1 or 2.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="boolean">
  Whether playback from segments is enabled, i.e. whether the clickableSections player setting is anything other than "none".
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  The value of window\.location.href when the event was emitted.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  The value of document.referrer when the event was emitted.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  This will always be the string "1" but we may use this later, e.g. to run A/B tests.
</ParamField>

<ParamField type="string">
  The version of the NPM package as specified in the package.json file.
</ParamField>


# Changelog
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/changelog

BeyondWords product updates and announcements

<Update label="August 25th 2026" description="Platform update">
  ### BeyondWords MCP

  With the BeyondWords MCP, you can configure and manage your BeyondWords account, projects, and content directly within compatible AI assistants.

  * Connect to Claude, ChatGPT, Cursor, VS Code, and other compatible AI apps
  * Ask your assistant to review analytics, update player settings, add pronunciation rules, and more
  * Connect securely through OAuth, choose read-only or write access, and keep your data private

  To get started, go to [Connected apps](https://dash.beyondwords.io/account/connections) in your BeyondWords profile.
</Update>

<Update label="August 12th 2026" description="WordPress update">
  ### WordPress plugin v7.0.0

  A redesigned editor experience and a simplified settings page.

  * New **Content**, **Format**, **Voice**, and **Player** sections in both the block and classic editors
  * Post previews now wait for processing to finish instead of showing a broken player
  * Opt-in **Customize** toggle for per-post language and voice overrides
  * Simplified plugin settings—audio/video generation and player display are managed from your BeyondWords project settings

  <Warning>
    **Breaking change**: the plugin no longer sends `metadata` to the BeyondWords API. If you're hooking `beyondwords_content_params` to write `$params['metadata']`, update it to append to `$params['tags']` instead, or your site will throw a fatal error on save.
  </Warning>
</Update>

<Update label="August 11th 2026" description="Content update">
  ### Tags

  Organize your content with tags—a simple way to label content items and power playlists and podcast feeds.

  * Add tags to any content item in the Editor's **Info** tab, or via the API
  * Build playlists and podcast feeds using tag-based rules (e.g., `tags` **include** or **exclude** a given value)
  * Custom JSON metadata (properties) is now deprecated. It's still supported for existing integrations, but can no longer be used to power playlists or podcast feeds—use tags instead.
</Update>

<Update label="July 15th 2026" description="Video update">
  ### Play video segments in the background

  You can now set video segments to play in the background of [generated videos](/docs-and-guides/content/video), instead of playing as standalone clips.

  * Enable **Play in background** on any video segment in the [Editor](/docs-and-guides/tools/editor#video-assets)
  * Via the API, set `segments.video_settings.plays_in_background` to `true` on video segments
    * Background clips follow the visual timing setting for your [style template](/docs-and-guides/content/video#style-templates)
</Update>

<Update label="June 8th 2026" description="Player update">
  ### Access tiers

  You can now customize the player experience based on the visitor's access tier.

  * Use access tiers to align the player experience with your paywall or membership model
  * Control how much content each tier can access
  * Enable or disable ads per tier
  * Set custom intro and outro audio per tier

  To configure access tiers, go to **Settings** **→** **Distribution → Player → Access tiers** in your project dashboard.

  [Learn more in our access tiers doc **→**](/docs-and-guides/distribution/player/access-tiers)
</Update>

<Update label="April 29th 2026" description="Voice update">
  ### Voice generation

  BeyondWords now offers [voice generation](/docs-and-guides/voices/voice-generation), so you can create custom AI voices from simple text prompts.

  * Create custom voices tailored by persona, emotion, tone, and more
  * No need for audio recordings or approvals
  * Generate multilingual voices in up to 74 languages

  To generate a voice, go to "Custom voices" in your organization settings.

  ### Professional voice cloning

  You can now create [Professional voice clones](/docs-and-guides/voices/voice-cloning) directly in the BeyondWords dashboard.

  * Create the highest-quality, most realistic voice clones
  * Requires 30+ minutes of clean audio from a single speaker, plus a verification statement
  * Ready in \~24 hours

  To clone a voice, go to "Custom voices" in your organization settings.
</Update>

<Update label="April 23rd 2026" description="Playlists update">
  ### Outros in playlists

  You can now control whether your outro plays after each item in a playlist, or just once after the last item in a playlist.

  Update your settings in the **Distribution → Player** section of your project dashboard or use the `outroPlaybackMode` parameter.
</Update>

<Update label="March 30th 2026" description="Player update">
  ### Continuous playback mode

  If you enable continuous playback, the BeyondWords Player will automatically play your most recent audio/video items after the current item ends (from newest to oldest, without repetition).

  This allows users to keep listening or watching uninterrupted.

  To enable continuous playback, go to **Distribution → Player → Interactions** in your project dashboard.
</Update>

<Update label="March 16th 2026" description="Product update">
  ### Voice model selection

  You can now switch between ElevenLabs voice models when using compatible ElevenLabs voices in BeyondWords.

  * Choose between Turbo v2.5, Multilingual v2, and v3 models (where available)
  * Select the best voice and model combination to suit your needs
  * Update voice settings or copy voice IDs in **Content → Preferences → Voices**

  Need help selecting the right voice? [Contact our team](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support).
</Update>

<Update label="March 12th 2026" description="Player update">
  ### Word highlighting

  Enable word highlighting to highlight individual words in your article as they're read aloud, making it easier for readers to follow along.

  * Can be used with paragraph highlighting or on its own
  * Set different colors for light mode and dark mode
  * Update your Project settings in **Distribution → Player**
</Update>

<Update label="February 19th 2026" description="Product updates">
  ### Pauses

  Fine-tune pacing by adding custom pauses to audio and video.

  * Use the Editor to manually insert pauses after specific words or segments
  * Add default pauses after titles or segments in **Project → Settings → General**
  * Define pause durations in seconds

  ***

  ### Vertical video support

  Automatically or manually embed vertical videos (9:16) using the BeyondWords Player.

  * Add `videoSizes: ['9:16']` to your embed script to enable in an existing embed configuration
  * Download individual video embed codes from the dashboard

  Want to explore how vertical video could enhance your strategy? [Reach out to our team](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support).
</Update>

<Update label="January 19th 2026" description="Summarization update">
  ### Script Templates (formerly Summarization)

  We've renamed Summarization to Script Templates, giving you more control over how articles are rewritten for audio and video.

  Instead of only summarizing content, BeyondWords can now adapt articles into different storytelling formats designed for listening and watching. Script Templates work seamlessly with both audio and video, helping you create content that fits the platform and audience.

  **What's new:**

  * **Summaries → Scripts**: The feature has been renamed to reflect its broader purpose—transforming articles into scripts, not just summaries.
  * **Script Templates**: Choose from our default templates to control structure, tone, and length before audio and video are generated, or create your own custom template.
  * **Built for video & audio**: Scripts are optimized for spoken delivery and visual storytelling, improving performance in both audio and video formats.
  * **Premade storytelling formats**: Use ready-to-go templates like Summary, Hook & Payoff, and Presenter Voiceover for different engagement goals and platforms.
  * **Custom templates**: Create your own instructions to match your editorial style, brand voice, or video strategy.
  * **Works with Style Templates**: Pair Script Templates with Style Templates to control both the narrative and the visual presentation of your videos.

  Scripts can be enabled automatically for new content or applied manually on an article-by-article basis, giving you flexibility while scaling audio and video output.

  Want to explore how this could benefit your team's workflow? Feel free to [reach out](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support) or have a read of our [Script documentation](/docs-and-guides/tools/generate-scripts) to get started.
</Update>

<Update label="December 18th 2025" description="Video update">
  ### Video 2.0 (Beta)—Scalable video for every newsroom

  We've launched an update to our video feature in BeyondWords, making it even easier to turn articles into engaging, branded videos at scale. Visit the blog to see [article-to-video demos](https://beyondwords.io/blog/introducing-scalable-video-for-every-newsroom/) or learn how to get started in our [video docs](/docs-and-guides/content/video).

  **What's new:**

  * **Style Templates**: Videos are generated using Style Templates that control layout and branding, ensuring consistent styling across all videos.
  * **Enhanced caption animations**: New caption styles and animations to improve readability and engagement.
  * **Enhanced media animations**: Improved transitions and motion effects for both images and video clips.
  * **Multiple formats**: Generate both vertical and horizontal videos, with image resizing to support different platforms.
  * **Video segments**: Articles can now include video clips as well as images, allowing generated videos to combine both media types within a single video.
  * **Featured Video**: Set a feature video to have a video—rather than images—play in the background of your video

  Video can be enabled [automatically](/docs-and-guides/content/video) for new content or generated [manually](/docs-and-guides/content/video) on an article-by-article basis.

  <Note>
    Please [reach out](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support) if you'd like any assistance testing this or setting it up.
  </Note>
</Update>

<Update label="November 26th 2025" description="Voice update">
  ### ElevenLabs voices now available

  * We've added 91 new [**ElevenLabs voices**](https://beyondwords.io/blog/elevenlabs-ai-voices-voice-cloning-live-in-beyondwords/) to the dashboard.
  * Available across 20+ languages

  <Note>
    Feel free to [reach out](/docs-and-guides/support/get-support) if you have any questions or would like personalized recommendations.
  </Note>

  ***

  ### New voice settings

  Gain finer control over the AI voices you use in BeyondWords. To update a voice's settings, go to **Content → Preferences → Voices** in your dashboard and select `…` → `Settings` alongside the voice.

  * **Multilingual mode**: Automatically detects foreign words and switches languages when needed.
  * **Speaking rate**: Adjust the voice's speaking speed.
  * **Expressiveness**: Controls how varied and emotional the delivery sounds.  *(Available on Azure HD and ElevenLabs voices)*
    * Lower values = steadier, more consistent
    * Higher values = more emotive and varied, but less consistent
  * **Similarity**: Determines how closely the output matches the original speaker's tone and character. *(Available on ElevenLabs voices)*
  * **Style**: Controls how stylized or expressive the delivery is compared to the original speaker. *(Available on ElevenLabs voices)*
  * **Speaker boost**: Enhances clarity and presence to better match the original speaker. *(Available on ElevenLabs voices)*

  <Note>
    **Similarity**, **Style**, and **Speaker Boost** work best when used with professional voice clones, as these settings are designed to enhance high-quality voice recordings.
  </Note>
</Update>

# Audio
Source: https://docs.beyondwords.io/docs-and-guides/content/audio



BeyondWords transforms written articles into high-quality audio delivered across your websites, apps, and third-party platforms. So you can tap into listening needs and preferences, unlock new engagement opportunities, and improve accessibility.

Design a fully automated workflow to:

* Convert published articles into audio using advanced [AI voices](/docs-and-guides/voices/overview)
* Embed audio into articles with features like [word highlighting](/docs-and-guides/distribution/player/player-introduction) and click-to-play
* Turn articles into specialized [scripts](/docs-and-guides/tools/generate-scripts), then convert them into audio
* Distribute audio through [playlists](/docs-and-guides/distribution/playlists) and [podcast feeds](/docs-and-guides/distribution/podcast-feeds)

<Tip>
  You can also generate and distribute [video content](/docs-and-guides/content/video).
</Tip>

## Generate audio

To generate audio, you must first create a [content item](/docs-and-guides/content/content-items). You can do this through an [integration](/docs-and-guides/integrations/integrations-overview), the Editor, or by [importing from URL](/docs-and-guides/tools/import-from-url).

Each content item includes an **article** (the main piece of text), which is automatically converted into an audio article.

You can optionally [create a script](/docs-and-guides/tools/generate-scripts) (an alternate version of the article) and convert it into an audio summary.

### Generate audio for scripts

To generate audio for scripts by default:

1. Go to **Content → Preferences → Script** in your project dashboard
2. Enable **Convert scripts into audio**
3. Click **Save changes**
4. Create [content items](/docs-and-guides/content/content-items) as usual

To generate audio for a specific script only:

1. Open or create your script in the [Editor](/docs-and-guides/tools/editor)
2. Go to the **Script** section in the sidebar
3. Enable **Convert script into audio**
4. **Generate** or **Regenerate** your content

## Customize audio

<CardGroup>
  <Card title="Text" icon="text" href="/docs-and-guides/tools/editor">
    The text  in your article and script are used to generate spoken-word audio.
  </Card>

  <Card title="Voices" icon="microphone-stand" href="/docs-and-guides/voices/overview">
    Choose [premade](/docs-and-guides/voices/premade-voices), [cloned](/docs-and-guides/voices/voice-cloning), or [generated](/docs-and-guides/voices/voice-generation) voices for your audio.
  </Card>

  <Card title="Customize speech" icon="gear" href="/docs-and-guides/voices/customization">
    Customize the speech output with pronunciation rules, pauses, and more.
  </Card>

  <Card title="Background music" icon="music" href="/docs-and-guides/tools/add-music">
    Insert background music to add atmosphere to your audio.
  </Card>

  <Card title="Insert audio" icon="file-audio" href="/docs-and-guides/tools/editor#audio-assets">
    Use the Editor to insert audio clips into your generated audio.
  </Card>

  <Card title="Intro and outro" icon="music-note" href="/docs-and-guides/distribution/player/player-settings">
    Play a custom intro and/or outro through the BeyondWords player.
  </Card>
</CardGroup>

<Warning>
  The audio generated for your [audio article](/docs-and-guides/content/audio) is also used in your [video article](/docs-and-guides/content/video). Likewise, the audio generated for your [audio summary](/docs-and-guides/tools/generate-scripts) is used in your [video summary](/docs-and-guides/tools/generate-scripts). This means any changes to the audio will affect both formats.
</Warning>
